About AI discoveries in 2023

This selection contains 191 catalog entries dated 3 Jan 2023 to 30 Dec 2023. 191 include structured evidence annotations describing reported tests, the AI contribution and remaining limits. Missing annotations mean those details have not been structured here, not that a study lacks evidence.

Dates follow the catalog publication policy. Coverage is selected and incomplete; entries can overlap or describe several results. Counts do not measure independent discoveries, clinical benefit or worldwide research output. Read the selection and evidence policy or compare catalog coverage by year and field.

Discoveries

30 Dec 2023Shandong University; Shandong First Medical University

MRI edema grades improve retrospective neural-network prognosis for Guillain–Barré syndrome

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A two-institution retrospective study used a shallow neural network to estimate residual symptoms six months after discharge in 354 Guillain–Barré syndrome patients. Adding radiologist-assigned lumbosacral edema grades to clinical variables increased the source-reported external-cohort AUC from 0.84 to 0.97. The model includes a muscle-strength score measured at discharge, so it is not an admission-only forecast. External testing used 118 patients from a second institution; treatment benefit and prospective deployment were not tested.

Technology: Shallow neural network

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Original source Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A shallow neural network learned six-month outcome classification from clinical factors, with and without radiologist-assigned MRI edema grades; comparison isolates the added predictive information in the MRI grade.
Test and result
Retrospective development on 236 patients and external testing on 118; source reports external AUC 0.84 for clinical variables versus 0.97 with MRI grading. MRI reader agreement was assessed separately.
Limitation / still unknown
The predictor includes discharge muscle strength and does not establish early admission-only utility. Retrospective selection and source reporting inconsistencies limit interpretation; no causal or treatment benefit is inferred.

28 Dec 2023Universidad ECCI

Machine learning screens cinnamic-acid derivatives for Leishmania enzyme inhibition

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Two random-forest classifiers and docking ranked an in-house set of cinnamic-acid derivatives; selected compounds were tested against recombinant L. major DHFR-TS. The paper reports IC50 values of 7.5, 6.1 and 10.1 µM for three named natural products versus 1.4 µM for methotrexate. These are enzyme assays, not whole-parasite treatment results.

Technology: Random-forest classifiers; structure-based docking

Editorial relevance 60 chemistry Original source Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
RF models and docking prioritize a small compound set for biochemical enzyme assays.
Test and result
Primary article reports recombinant DHFR-TS inhibition for selected compounds and comparator.
Limitation / still unknown
Methods/results disagree on number of compounds; name/formula spellings and exact prior art need checking.

28 Dec 2023Kyushu University; Tokyo Women’s Medical University

Learned PET-like SPECT correction improves some regional scores but can erase true perfusion defects

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A learned image-translation model was evaluated against PET in forty held-out cardiac-imaging patients. It reduced some inferior-wall SPECT scoring discrepancies, but also produced overcorrection that erased a true defect in an illustrated case. The result supports a limited retrospective imaging validation with explicit failure modes, without proof of improved clinical diagnosis or safe replacement of standard imaging.

Technology: Adversarial cycle-consistent SPECT-to-PET image translation with a U-Net generator

Editorial relevance 40 biology Original source Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The learned adversarial translation transformed non-attenuation-corrected SPECT images into PET-like representations, enabling the represented patient-level comparison of regional attenuation artifacts and correction failures against an imaging reference.
Test and result
Seventy-one patients had matched SPECT/PET examinations. Thirty-one supplied image-level training/validation data; forty different patients supplied the clinical test set. Two radiologists and one cardiologist performed blinded, randomized-order consensus scoring against PET. Regional inferior-wall overscoring was reduced in the translated images, while the source documented an example of a real perfusion defect disappearing after correction.
Limitation / still unknown
Internal training/validation images were randomly split within thirty-one patients, rather than a demonstrated patient-separated validation split. Training SPECT/PET scans could be separated by up to three years; test scans were separated by roughly forty-six days on average. Single-center selection, small and biased clinical subgroups, consensus readers and reference-scan temporal differences limit generalization. Some image-error metrics worsened and reduced significance against PET is not proof of equivalence. True-defect erasure prevents any claim of uniformly safe correction. No clinical outcome, radiation-dose benefit or replacement utility was established.
Evidence sources and annotation context

Evidence context (descriptions follow the cited sources; test type is a catalog classification):

Evidence sources: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10884131/

27 Dec 2023University Hospital Basel; PulseAI

Neural ECG classifier reduces inconclusive readings across four devices

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A prospective diagnostic study compared a residual neural classifier with four device algorithms against physician-interpreted ECGs. Among 824 recordings from 206 patients, inconclusive results fell from 16% to 1.2%. This selected hospital study does not establish population-screening or workload benefit.

Technology: PulseAI residual convolutional neural network

Editorial relevance 47 medicine Original source Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A trained residual CNN classifies rhythm from raw single-lead signals.
Test and result
Near-contemporaneous physician-interpreted 12-lead references; blinded adjudication. Training contained no smart-device recordings.
Limitation / still unknown
52 of 258 recruited patients lacked complete recordings. Selected electrophysiology patients, vectorized exports and unrepeated inconclusive recordings limit generalization. Four recordings per participant are correlated. Vendor-affiliated authors participated; no clinical-outcome benefit tested.
Evidence sources and annotation context

25 Dec 2023University of Minnesota

Video transformers quantify eye tics in adolescents with Tourette syndrome

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A transformer classified eye-tic video segments from 11 adolescents with Tourette syndrome. Performance fell from an AUC of about 0.90 under random splitting to about 0.75 when patients were separated between training and validation. Validation data were monitored during optimization, so this is limited internal diagnostic measurement evidence rather than an untouched external test, a Tourette diagnosis or demonstrated clinical benefit.

Technology: Transformer; computer-vision facial landmarks

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Original source Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A shallow transformer learns temporal sequences of facial landmarks to identify manually annotated eye-tic events.
Test and result
Manually labeled eye-tic segments and patient-separated validation, alongside a random-split comparison.
Limitation / still unknown
External patient testing, broader tic recognition and clinical utility remain unestablished; monitored validation is not a pristine test set.

24 Dec 2023Technical University of Munich; Saarland University Medical Center; University Hospital Schleswig-Holstein; Robert Bosch Hospital

Random forests link CT features to catheter-measured endpoints in CTEPH

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Random forests combined CT measurements and qualitative findings to classify catheter-measured hemodynamic endpoints in patients with operable chronic thromboembolic pulmonary hypertension. Retrospective internal testing reported AUC 0.82 for a pulmonary-pressure category and 0.74 for a pulmonary-artery oxygen-saturation category. Replacement of invasive testing or improved management was not demonstrated.

Technology: Random forest

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Original source Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Random forests learned combinations of CT-derived measurements and qualitative imaging findings to classify catheter-referenced hemodynamic categories; feature filtering characterized the model-relevant signals.
Test and result
Single-center retrospective cohort of 127 operable CTEPH patients from 2015–2020 with CT and preoperative right-heart catheterization. Pulmonary pressure was available in 127 patients and pulmonary-artery oxygen saturation in 55. Random forests used normalized quantitative and qualitative CT features, evaluated through a source-described threefold random-sampling procedure. Reported endpoint-classification AUCs were 0.82 and 0.74.
Limitation / still unknown
Small selected surgical cohort, including only 55 patients for the oxygen endpoint; no external model validation. Exact normalization/filtering nesting and hyperparameter selection were incompletely specified. CT-feature associations are observational, and feature importance is not causation. Benefits for patient management and replacement of catheterization remain untested.
Evidence sources and annotation context

Evidence context (descriptions follow the cited sources; test type is a catalog classification):

22 Dec 2023Massachusetts Institute of Technology; collaborating institutions

Machine-learning-guided closed-loop synthesis experimentally realizes unreported dye molecules

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A closed-loop molecular discovery platform combined machine-learning property prediction, automated synthesis and characterization, and iterative model updates to explore dye-like molecules. In the published Science abstract, the team reports experimentally realizing 294 previously unreported molecules across three design–make–test–analyze cycles on four rarely reported scaffolds, while also identifying six top-performing molecules in a second case study using pre-existing chemical-space examples.

Technology: Autonomous molecular-discovery platform integrating generative and property-prediction machine-learning models, Bayesian optimization, and synthesis-planning software with automated chemical synthesis and characterization

Editorial relevance 60 chemistry Original source Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Generative ML proposed candidate dye-like structures; property-prediction models ranked molecules against absorption, lipophilicity, and photo-oxidative-stability targets; automated multi-step synthesis and measurements generated training data for model retraining and the next cycles. The closed-loop process converted model-guided suggestions into synthesized and characterized compounds.
Test and result
In the published Science abstract, the team reports experimentally realizing 294 previously unreported molecules across three design–make–test–analyze cycles on four rarely reported scaffolds, while also identifying six top-performing molecules in a second case study using pre-existing chemical-space examples.
Limitation / still unknown
This is molecular discovery in dye-like chemical systems, not a validated drug or biological effect. The 294 number is from the published Science abstract; an earlier ChemRxiv preprint describes 312 unreported molecules, so version-specific counts differ and the published figure is used here. The measurements addressed absorption wavelength, lipophilicity and photo-oxidative stability; no environmental, toxicological or commercial benefit is claimed. Primary-source abstract and author-lab description checked; independent replication not assessed.

22 Dec 2023Shanghai Jiao Tong University School of Medicine; University of Shanghai for Science and Technology

DNN stratifies kidney cancer survival using ten cuproptosis-related genes

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A deep neural network trained on ten cuproptosis-related gene-expression features stratified kidney clear-cell cancer survival in TCGA and was evaluated in an external ICGC dataset. The authors report AUCs of 0.739 in training and 0.707 in validation. This was retrospective database analysis; proposed drug-target interactions remain computational predictions and clinical benefit was not tested.

Technology: Deep neural network

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Original source Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A three-hidden-layer DNN trained on ten cuproptosis-related expression features in TCGA predicts KIRC survival with external ICGC evaluation.
Test and result
Retrospective TCGA survival modeling and external ICGC evaluation.
Limitation / still unknown
Prospective performance, clinical benefit and predicted drug-target mechanisms were not established.

22 Dec 2023University Medical Center Hamburg-Eppendorf; Jung Diagnostics

Neural uncertainty flags difficult dopamine transporter scans

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Residual-network ensembles classified dopamine-transporter SPECT and flagged uncertain decisions. A separate 645-scan Parkinson progression dataset showed 98.0% binary classification accuracy against clinical diagnosis; the uncertainty module flagged 92.3% of classification errors. Performance varied across camera datasets. This retrospective evaluation does not establish improved patient management or reliable exclusion of neurodegeneration in individual borderline scans.

Technology: Residual convolutional neural-network ensembles

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Original source Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Separate high-sensitivity and high-specificity learned ensembles flag disagreement around an independently generated image classification.
Test and result
Development on 1,250 scans, separate 490-scan development test, 640-scan camera test, and 645-scan external clinical-reference test.
Limitation / still unknown
Prospective clinical utility remains unestablished; reference labels differ across cohorts, camera shifts affect performance, and uncertainty is binary rather than a calibrated probability.
Evidence sources and annotation context

21 Dec 2023University of Science and Technology Beijing Institute for Advanced Materials and Technology; National Materials Corrosion and Protection Data Center; Beijing University of Chemical Technology

Machine learning discovers D-amino-acid mixtures that boost anti-biofilm antibiotic cocktails

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A machine-learning-guided high-throughput screen identified a five-component D-amino-acid mixture (“D-mix”) with about 78% anti-biofilm activity against Pseudomonas aeruginosa at 100 μM. Combining D-mix with half the lowest effective gentamicin dose retained about 91% bacterial-growth inhibition and about 93% anti-biofilm efficacy in vitro. In a small mouse lung-infection model, gentamicin/D-mix treatment reduced measured P. aeruginosa burden by about 95% in blood and 91% in lung tissue at 72 hours.

Technology: High-throughput screening coupled to machine-learning regression and iterative global optimization of D-amino-acid mixture ratios

Editorial relevance 60 medicine Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Experimental measurements of mixture biofilm effects were used to train and iteratively update ML regression models, which guided mixture selection over design rounds. The selected D-mix was then evaluated with different antibiotics in vitro; the lead gentamicin/D-mix combination was tested in a P. aeruginosa lung-infection mouse model.
Test and result
In a small mouse lung-infection model, gentamicin/D-mix treatment reduced measured P. aeruginosa burden by about 95% in blood and 91% in lung tissue at 72 hours.
Limitation / still unknown
The in-vivo experiment used a small mouse P. aeruginosa infection model, not human treatment; study-specific bacterial counts and efficacy are reported at 72 hours. The composition is a selected mixture of five D-amino acids, with authors noting the full synergism mechanism remains unclear. D-mix alone was not bactericidal under the reported in-vitro conditions; effects are combination- and assay-dependent.
Evidence sources and annotation context

20 Dec 2023MIT

A new class of antibiotic candidates against MRSA

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A deep-learning model trained on about 39,000 compounds screened about 12 million commercial compounds; of about 280 purchased and tested, two from the same class reduced MRSA burden tenfold in two mouse models, with very low toxicity to human cells. Results from plates and mice; toxicity was partly predicted computationally.

Technology: Deep learning with chemical-structure explainability

Editorial relevance 60 medicine Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Graph neural network ensembles trained on about 39,000 compounds with measured antibacterial and human-cell toxicity predicted both properties for about 12 million compounds and flagged candidates for purchase and testing.
Test and result
About 283 compounds were bought and tested empirically; one structural class reduced bacterial burden in mouse skin and systemic thigh infection models, and cytotoxicity to human cells was measured.
Limitation / still unknown
The page does not list specific study limitations; next steps are chemical property analysis and potential clinical use.

20 Dec 2023Carnegie Mellon University; and Emerald Cloud Lab

Coscientist: autonomous chemical research with language models

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A GPT-4-guided system designs, plans and runs experiments using web search, documentation, code and laboratory automation, performing six tasks including optimization of palladium cross-coupling reactions. The abstract does not make clear which tasks used real hardware; only the abstract was reviewed.

Technology: Coscientist (GPT-4)

Family GPT-4: profile · models page

Editorial relevance 80 chemistry Original source Suggest a correction

18 Dec 2023Mengchao Hepatobiliary Hospital of Fujian Medical University; Fujian Cancer Hospital, Zhejiang University and collaborating Chinese clinical institutions

A machine-learning-selected P4 serum panel detects hepatocellular carcinoma and signals cirrhosis-to-cancer progression

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A staged serum-proteomics study across 1,002 individuals used random-forest machine learning to select a four-marker panel, P4 (HABP2, CD163, AFP and PIVKA-II). In an independent validation set, the panel distinguished hepatocellular carcinoma from cirrhosis (AUC 0.979; sensitivity 0.925; specificity 0.915) and from healthy controls (AUC 0.992). In a prospective external cohort of cirrhosis patients followed for HCC, it predicted conversion with AUC 0.890 and median lead time of 11.4 months before imaging diagnosis.

Technology: Random-forest machine-learning model for serum-protein panel selection and HCC classification

Editorial relevance 53 medicine Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The random-forest model evaluated serum protein panels and selected the four-marker P4 panel; researchers then tested classification in an independent validation set and assessed prediction of cirrhosis-to-HCC conversion in prospective follow-up data.
Test and result
In an independent validation set, the panel distinguished hepatocellular carcinoma from cirrhosis (AUC 0.979; sensitivity 0.925; specificity 0.915) and from healthy controls (AUC 0.992). The prospectively collected cirrhosis cohort was split into 177 training and 76 validation participants for predictive-model construction; collection timing alone does not establish a prospective locked-model evaluation.
Limitation / still unknown
The prospective cohort included 253 cirrhosis patients, with 36 developing HCC; 8 of 65 participants were false positives for conversion by the reported endpoint. This is source-reported research performance, not proof of improved survival or a clinically implemented screening test. The lead time is measured relative to imaging diagnosis in the study cohorts.

18 Dec 2023University of Washington, Department of Earth and Space Sciences

Machine-learning denoising exposes strong size scaling in deep-earthquake rupture parameters

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A multitask neural denoiser recovered teleseismic P-wave pulses from deep Mw 5.0+ earthquakes that had previously been too noisy for source analysis. The study found moment–duration scaling M₀ ∼ τ⁴, stress-drop scaling Δσ ∼ M₀⁰·²¹ and radiated-energy scaling Eᵣ ∼ M₀¹·²⁴; deep events had a median radiation efficiency near 5%, weak directivity and few subevents. After accounting for rigidity, no systematic depth trend in duration, stress drop or radiated energy remained across 100–700 km.

Technology: DenoTe, a transfer-learned multitask convolutional encoder–decoder neural network

Editorial relevance 53 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Separated low-SNR teleseismic P-wave signals from overlapping noise, enabling source-parameter estimation from 920 deep-earthquake records, including moderate events ordinarily omitted from these analyses.
Test and result
A multitask neural denoiser recovered teleseismic P-wave pulses from deep Mw 5.0+ earthquakes that had previously been too noisy for source analysis.
Limitation / still unknown
The study is a source-parameter reanalysis enabled by neural denoising, not prospective earthquake prediction. It reports scaling relations and interpretations from the available global event sample; stress and energy estimates remain subject to waveform, path-correction and source-model uncertainties.

5 Dec 2023Weill Cornell Medicine; New York Genome Center; collaborating institutions

3D-HiChAT predicts embryonic enhancer hubs validated by CRISPR interference

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A random-forest model integrating ATAC-seq, H3K27ac ChIP-seq and HiChIP data predicted enhancer effects across mouse trophoblast stem cells, embryonic stem cells and extraembryonic endoderm cells. CRISPRi tests supported a shared Enh14 hub regulating both Tfcp2l1 and Gli2 in embryonic stem cells, and an Enh4 hub linked to seven genes in extraembryonic endoderm; four of those seven showed expression reductions as predicted. Across 40 tested enhancer-promoter pairs, experiments yielded 12 true positives and 13 true negatives, with overall prediction accuracy 0.71.

Technology: 3D-HiChAT, an optimized Random Forest model combining chromatin accessibility, H3K27ac and 3D HiChIP connectivity features

Editorial relevance 60 biology Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The model used 1D and 3D chromatin features to rank genome-wide enhancer-promoter pairs by predicted expression impact after in-silico enhancer perturbation. Researchers selected predictions for CRISPRi validation and measured target-gene expression by RT-qPCR; the paper reports specific validated multi-gene enhancer hubs and positive/negative prediction rates across 40 pairs.
Test and result
CRISPRi tests supported a shared Enh14 hub regulating both Tfcp2l1 and Gli2 in embryonic stem cells, and an Enh4 hub linked to seven genes in extraembryonic endoderm; four of those seven showed expression reductions as predicted.
Limitation / still unknown
The experiments were performed in mouse-derived cell lines, not intact embryos or human cells. Only selected predictions were tested; the 40-pair validation had 12 true positives, 13 true negatives and an overall reported accuracy of 0.71, leaving discordant/uncertain pairs. The Enh14 and Enh4 results support specific cell-type-dependent regulatory hubs but do not establish the full genome-wide predicted map as experimentally validated or prove developmental phenotypes in vivo.

2 Dec 2023ETH Zurich; Institute of Microbiology

Machine learning identifies bacterial strains and combinations that reduce pathogen colonization in Arabidopsis

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Random forest and elastic-net models analyzed 136 synthetic communities, each composed of five bacterial strains, and identified strain identity as the strongest predictor of protection against Pseudomonas syringae DC3000 in Arabidopsis. Independent test communities confirmed predictive performance, and follow-up plant assays showed that Acidovorax Leaf76, Rhizobium Leaf68 and Pseudomonas Leaf15 each reduced pathogen colonization by about two orders of magnitude versus axenic controls; a Rhizobium Leaf371–Arthrobacter Leaf337 combination also reduced colonization.

Technology: Random forest and elastic-net generalized linear models (GLMNet)

Editorial relevance 60 biology Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Models predicted pathogen outcomes from strain presence or abundance across the 136 screened communities and ranked strains by feature importance. Researchers tested the leading strains and combinations in additional plant experiments, validating the model-prioritized protection results.
Test and result
Independent test communities confirmed predictive performance, and follow-up plant assays showed that Acidovorax Leaf76, Rhizobium Leaf68 and Pseudomonas Leaf15 each reduced pathogen colonization by about two orders of magnitude versus axenic controls; a Rhizobium Leaf371–Arthrobacter Leaf337 combination also reduced colonization.
Limitation / still unknown
The experiments used gnotobiotic Arabidopsis thaliana and P. syringae DC3000, not field crops. Validation was in the same study, with 24 plants per treatment in the key individual-strain validation assay. The work establishes protection in this model system; it does not establish the underlying mechanisms, efficacy in other plants or field performance.

Deep learning detects slow slip events in Cascadia GNSS data

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A detector combining convolutional and attention-based neural networks, trained on synthetic geodetic time series, was applied to raw GNSS data in Cascadia from 2007 to 2022. The authors report 78 detections, including 35 of 40 events in the 2007–2017 comparison catalog and 43 additional events. Detection patterns align with tremor activity, but the paper says independent acquisition-data validation is needed, and shorter/low-confidence events remain uncertain. This method is specific to this station network and does not predict earthquakes.

Explainable regression guides laboratory tests of malaria candidates

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Piecewise-regression models learned activity relationships from 386 Open Source Malaria compounds and prioritized fragments for a commercial-library search. Researchers tested 22 selected compounds and followed three in concentration-response and human-cell cytotoxicity assays. A02 and A10 showed qualitatively consistent parasite-growth inhibition across the initial screen and follow-up curves. The third candidate, A22, has an unresolved screen-versus-curve discrepancy. These are modest-potency laboratory starting points, without established treatment efficacy or secure strongest-hit and selectivity claims.

Finding: PANDA was trained on non-contrast abdominal CT scans from 3,208 patients at a high-volume pancreatic cancer centre in Shanghai. Finding: Ng et al. prospectively assessed a commercial AI system as an additional reader beside standard double reading in three rollout phases (about 25,000 women, four sites), finding 0.7-1.6 additional cancers per 1,000 cases, 0.16-0.30 percent additional recalls and a 0.1-1.9 percent gain in positive predictive value after 7-11 percent additional human reads. 6 Nov 2023Google DeepMind; Imperial College London; Yonsei University; University of Bonn; Worcester Polytechnic Institute

AlphaZero finds improved finite extremal-graph bounds

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A neural network-guided AlphaZero search with a curriculum generated triangle- and 4-cycle-free graphs that improve known lower bounds for the extremal function ex(n,{C3,C4}) over finite sizes; the proceedings report AlphaZero improvements for n=64–136 and release the graph constructions. The paper also finds improvements using a separate tabu-search method, which often matches or outperforms AlphaZero. This does not solve the asymptotic Erdős conjecture. The result was present in the arXiv v1 posted 2023-11-06; the 2024 IJCAI proceedings paper is a later version.

Technology: AlphaZero reinforcement learning with neural Pairformer representation and curriculum

Editorial relevance 53 mathematics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
AlphaZero with a neural Pairformer representation and a curriculum generated extremal graphs improving the established lower bounds for n=64–136; tabu search is a separate, non-neural method and its results are not attributed to AI.
Test and result
The paper reports released graphs with improved lower bounds for ex(n,{C3,C4}) for node counts 64–134. Incremental AlphaZero (neural network-guided Monte Carlo tree search) is explicitly reported to improve bounds for sizes 64–136 in the proceedings version; incremental tabu search also improves bounds and the authors report it can match or exceed AlphaZero. This is a finite-graph construction result, not a proof of the asymptotic Erdős conjecture.
Limitation / still unknown
The result consists of explicit finite constructions/lower bounds; the asymptotic conjecture remains open. The work does not establish that learned search outperforms strong local search.

31 Oct 2023Imperial College London; University of Nottingham

Machine learning exposed a structure in terminal-singularity classification

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A neural-network classifier trained on eight-dimensional, Picard-rank-two toric varieties achieved 95% accuracy on a five-million-example test set and enabled rapid generation of a much larger predicted landscape of Q-Fano varieties. The authors report that the model’s classification pattern led them to formulate a new combinatorial criterion for terminal singularities, which they prove mathematically. The learned model suggested the route to the theorem; it did not supply the proof.

Technology: Feed-forward neural-network classifier trained on toric-variety weight matrices

Editorial relevance 60 mathematics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The trained neural-network classifier revealed a tractable pattern in the classification landscape and directly motivated the authors’ new combinatorial criterion; the authors separately establish its correctness by proof.
Test and result
The authors report a 95% classification accuracy on a balanced test split of five million eight-dimensional toric-variety examples and use the model to explore a larger Q-Fano landscape. They formulate and prove a new combinatorial criterion for terminal singularities, motivated by the ML analysis.
Limitation / still unknown
The 95% is internal held-out classification performance, not independent external validation; the authors state the neural network does not itself perform the theorem’s divisibility-based test or proof.

30 Oct 2023Linköping University; University of Skövde; Karolinska Institutet and Karolinska University Hospital; collaborating Swedish institutions

Machine-learning models identify cerebrospinal-fluid protein signatures for multiple-sclerosis activity and disability progression

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Using Olink proteomics of 1,463 proteins in CSF and plasma from two prospective Swedish cohorts, the study trained prediction models for multiple-sclerosis diagnosis, 2-year disease activity and long-term disability worsening. Lower CSF neurofilament light (NfL) predicted absence of disease activity at two years (replication AUC 0.77). A stepwise model using 11 CSF proteins (CXCL13, LTA, FCN2, ICAM3, LY9, SLAMF7, TYMP, CHI3L1, FYB1, TNFRSF1B and NfL) predicted normalized age-related MS severity; subgroup classification AUCs in the replication cohort ranged from 0.88 to 0.90.

Technology: Stepwise logistic regression for diagnosis and disease activity; stepwise linear regression for long-term disability severity

Editorial relevance 53 medicine Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The study used statistical differential-expression testing to select disease-associated candidate proteins, then fitted logistic-regression models to predict MS diagnosis and 2-year disease activity and a linear-regression model to predict long-term normalized age-related MS severity. Stepwise feature selection produced the protein combinations; the frozen models were evaluated in a separate prospective cohort.
Test and result
Using Olink proteomics of 1,463 proteins in CSF and plasma from two prospective Swedish cohorts, the study trained prediction models for multiple-sclerosis diagnosis, 2-year disease activity and long-term disability worsening.
Limitation / still unknown
The study included 143 people with early-stage MS and 43 healthy controls across discovery and replication cohorts; the replication MS cohort had 51 participants. The 2-year disease-activity comparison in replication was especially small (45 with activity versus 5 without). The 11-protein severity model predicted observational disability scores rather than treatment benefit. The plasma severity model had only 12 eligible replication samples and performed less convincingly. These are cohort-level results, not a clinically validated diagnostic assay or independent replication by another research group.

25 Oct 2023University of Warwick; University Hospitals Coventry and Warwickshire NHS Foundation Trust, collaborating UK hospitals and IMP Diagnostics (Portugal)

CAIMAN validates AI prescreening of typical and atypical large-bowel biopsies across UK and Portuguese cohorts

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: CAIMAN is a weakly supervised deep-learning model trained on pathologist-labeled whole-slide images to classify typical, atypical non-neoplastic and atypical neoplastic bowel biopsies. In three external cohorts comprising 1,536 slides from the UK and Portugal, it detected atypical biopsies with AUROC values of 0.9431, 0.9576 and 0.9636. At 99% sensitivity, it outperformed the prior IDaRS pipeline on two external cohorts, but not the third.

Technology: CAIMAN (Colorectal AI Model for Abnormality Detection), a weakly supervised deep-learning whole-slide image classifier

Editorial relevance 47 medicine Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

The trained frozen model was evaluated on three external cohorts; the reported finding is a specific retrospective clinical diagnostic-validation result.
Test and result
In three external cohorts comprising 1,536 slides from the UK and Portugal, it detected atypical biopsies with AUROC values of 0.9431, 0.9576 and 0.9636.
Limitation / still unknown
Retrospective validation on archived biopsies does not establish clinical deployment or improved patient outcomes. CAIMAN did not outperform IDaRS at 99% sensitivity in one of the three external validation cohorts; the authors call for larger multicentre validation and a clinical trial. The reported 99% sensitivity is paired with cohort-specific specificity, not a universal performance guarantee.

24 Oct 2023University of Cambridge; and Cambridge University Hospitals; Barts Health NHS Trust; Medical University of Vienna; collaborating institutions

Integrated radiogenomic machine learning predicts neoadjuvant chemotherapy response in metastatic ovarian cancer

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: The IRON ensemble combined baseline clinical data, CA-125, ctDNA and CT radiomics from primary and metastatic lesions to estimate tumor-volume change during neoadjuvant chemotherapy for high-grade serous ovarian carcinoma. In an independent 42-patient Barts cohort, the integrated model achieved AUC 0.78 for RECIST 1.1 response classification versus 0.47 for a clinical-only model; radiomics-containing scores significantly correlated with volume change in internal holdout. The study also measured spatial heterogeneity, with omental lesions showing better response than pelvic/ovarian disease.

Technology: IRON ensemble combining elastic net, support-vector regression and random forest

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Applying THOR to one eddy-permitting CM4 climate simulation, researchers detected a projected dynamical-regime shift where the Antarctic Circumpolar Current crosses the Pacific–Antarctic Ridge under the SSP5-8.5 scenario. Neural-network explanations and transport analysis attributed the modeled change to stronger wind stress shifting the current northward into less topographically constrained waters, where the flow intensifies. This is a model-based scenario result, not an observed future change.

6 Oct 2023École Polytechnique Fédérale de Lausanne; University of Liège; European Southern Observatory; Universidad Andrés Bello

CNN-assisted search confirms quadruply lensed quasar PS J2107−1611

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A convolutional neural network trained on simulated lens images screened 27,760 preselected quasar candidates with multiple nearby Pan-STARRS detections. Researchers visually inspected the top 2,000 scores and selected follow-up targets; spectroscopy and multi-survey imaging established PS J2107−1611 as a quadruply imaged quasar at redshift 2.673, with a 4.3-arcsecond image separation and a lensed host-galaxy arc. Its fold-image optical fluxes are roughly 15 times fainter than a smooth lens-mass model predicts, while radio flux ratios are consistent with that model. The date is the original preprint posting; the journal version appeared in November 2023.

Technology: Convolutional neural network trained on simulated quadruply lensed quasars, applied to Pan-STARRS gri image cutouts

Editorial relevance 53 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The CNN ranked image cutouts of 27,760 quasar catalog entries that already had multiple nearby Pan-STARRS detections. The team visually examined the top 2,000, selected 131 promising systems, and followed targets with imaging/spectroscopy; the model therefore enabled prioritization but did not itself confirm lensing. Human follow-up established the quasar redshift and lens configuration.
Test and result
A convolutional neural network trained on simulated lens images screened 27,760 preselected quasar candidates with multiple nearby Pan-STARRS detections.
Limitation / still unknown
This is a single confirmed system from a prefiltered candidate set; the authors visually reviewed high-scoring candidates and performed follow-up. Spectroscopy confirms the source is a quasar at z=2.673, while the four-image lens interpretation is supported by high-resolution imaging, lens modeling, a host-galaxy arc, and wavelength-dependent flux ratios. The ∼15-fold optical fold-pair anomaly is relative to the smooth macromodel; the cause is not resolved in this letter. Detailed JWST/VLT/EVN follow-up was left for future work.

26 Sep 2023Montera, Inc. (Forta)

Gradient-boosted trees distinguish historical autism diagnostic categories

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Gradient-boosted trees classified historical DSM-IV autism categories using assessment scores, demographics and comorbidities. In a person-separated SPARK test set, the multiclass model correctly classified 80.5% of individuals; 12.6% were assigned a different autism-spectrum category. This retrospective result does not establish screening performance under current DSM-5 criteria.

Technology: Gradient-boosted decision trees

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Original source Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Gradient-boosted trees learned associations between clinical/assessment features and recorded diagnostic categories, then classified unseen individuals from the same retrospective dataset.
Test and result
SPARK supplied 36,965 individuals, split 80:20 without person overlap; fivefold training-only grid search optimized gradient-boosted trees. The multiclass model correctly classified 5,951 of 7,393 test individuals (80.5%), with 933 assigned a different autism-spectrum category. Separate one-versus-reference models yielded AUROCs 0.863–0.980. ABIDE supported a separately developed model, not external testing of the SPARK model.
Limitation / still unknown
Historical professional DSM-IV diagnoses were the reference; absence of diagnostic codes defined non-spectrum controls. Assessment inputs overlap with information used in diagnostic evaluation, limiting independent screening interpretation. SPARK and ABIDE models used different inputs and were fitted separately. No prospective workflow, patient benefit or current DSM-5 diagnostic validation was established; PDD-NOS positive predictive value was low.

8 Sep 2023Imperial College London; University of Nottingham

Machine learning helps recover dimension from quantum periods for a class of Fano varieties

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A feed-forward neural network classified the dimension of Fano varieties from quantum-period coefficients with 98% accuracy on the authors’ dataset. The authors report that machine learning helped formulate rigorous asymptotic results, which prove dimension recovery for specified low-Picard-rank toric Fano classes. The broader conjecture that the quantum period uniquely determines every Fano variety remains open; the empirical classification accuracy is not itself a proof. The journal article appeared 2023-09-08, before the same work’s 2023-09-11 arXiv posting.

30 Aug 2023Chonnam National University; Pohang University of Science and Technology; University of Washington; University of Hawaiʻi at Mānoa; Institute for Basic Science; Pusan National University

Deep learning detects an emerging anthropogenic warming fingerprint in daily precipitation

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A convolutional neural network trained on 80 CESM2 large-ensemble members detected an emerging climate-change signal in observed daily precipitation fields. Across four satellite, merged-observation and reanalysis products, the model’s annual estimated global-mean temperature exceeded the historical internal-variability range from the mid-2010s onward. The signal was driven mainly by precipitation variability on timescales shorter than 10 days, with hotspots including the tropical eastern Pacific and mid-latitude storm tracks; rain-gauge checks over the eastern United States were consistent with the increase in high-frequency variability.

Technology: Deep detection (DD) convolutional neural network

Editorial relevance 53 climate Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The CNN was trained on paired daily precipitation anomaly maps and annual global-mean surface-air-temperature values from 80 CESM2 Large Ensemble members. Applied to observed precipitation products, it learned spatial and temporal patterns that predicted the warming metric and exposed an emergent signal that conventional linear detection did not resolve. Explainability analyses (occlusion sensitivity, with SHAP and integrated-gradients checks) localized the strongest contributions to high-frequency precipitation variability.
Test and result
A convolutional neural network trained on 80 CESM2 large-ensemble members detected an emerging climate-change signal in observed daily precipitation fields.
Limitation / still unknown
This is an observational detection and attribution result using a CNN trained on climate-model simulations; it is not a direct causal experiment or a forecast of individual storms. The long-term mean precipitation change remained indistinguishable from natural variability in the analysis. Precipitation products disagree in some estimates, especially where direct ocean measurements are sparse, and the reported hotspots depend on the selected datasets and attribution methods. The paper reports an emerging signal beginning in the mid-2010s, not a uniform increase in all regions or at every timescale.

21 Aug 2023University of Texas at Austin; Dell Medical School; Columbia University; New York Genome Center

Deep-learning knee imaging phenotypes reveal osteoarthritis genetic and fracture associations

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Using knee DXA scans from the UK Biobank, a ResNet-101 model identified 1,931 more probable knee-osteoarthritis cases than health-record coding and a U-Net segmentation model estimated minimum joint-space width (mJSW). In 29,257 participants, the quantitative mJSW phenotype had substantially higher SNP heritability than the binary phenotypes and yielded 18 genome-wide-significant loci, compared with one for the image-based binary classifier and six for ICD-10 coding; only one mJSW locus was already significant in a large prior knee-OA GWAS. The AI-derived mJSW phenotype was also associated with fracture history in an epidemiological analysis, a signal not seen with the binary case labels.

Technology: ResNet-101 convolutional neural network for knee-OA classification and U-Net image segmentation for joint-space measurement

Editorial relevance 53 biology Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The neural networks converted routine knee DXA scans into two disease phenotypes: a case–control OA classifier and a quantitative minimum joint-space-width measurement. The researchers used these AI-derived phenotypes in GWAS and downstream epidemiological association analyses, increasing statistical power and revealing genetic and fracture associations that were not significant with ICD-10 case coding or the binary classifier.
Test and result
Using knee DXA scans from the UK Biobank, a ResNet-101 model identified 1,931 more probable knee-osteoarthritis cases than health-record coding and a U-Net segmentation model estimated minimum joint-space width (mJSW).
Limitation / still unknown
The GWAS used 29,257 quality-controlled UK Biobank participants with knee DXA images, all from the analyzed white-British ancestry subset; ancestry transferability is unestablished. DXA images were non-weight-bearing. The 18 loci are genome-wide associations for a model-derived quantitative endophenotype, not 18 proven causal OA genes; the paper notes only one also reached significance in a previous large knee-OA GWAS and many loci overlap broad anthropometric associations. The fracture result is observational and does not show that OA or joint-space narrowing causes fractures. The medRxiv preprint DOI 10.1101/2023.03.07.23286909 is an earlier version of this same study and is not counted separately.

8 Aug 2023Google Research; American Airlines and Breakthrough Energy

Avoiding aircraft contrails with AI forecasts

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In a trial with American Airlines and Breakthrough Energy, pilots flew 70 flights over six months using AI forecasts to avoid forming contrails, reducing them by 54% compared with flights without the forecasts. Flights that avoided contrails used 2% more fuel; across an airline’s flights, the impact could fall to 0.3% if only a small share need rerouting.

2 Aug 2023Ames National Laboratory, U.S. Department of Energy; Iowa State University

Physics-informed machine learning guides experimental Curie-temperature validation in Ce–Zr–Fe alloys

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A machine-learning model trained on measured Curie temperatures selected two compositions in the (Zr1−xCex)Fe2 system, (Zr0.16Ce0.84)Fe2 and (Zr0.94Ce0.06)Fe2, for experimental validation. The study also reports a relationship between electronic states near the Fermi level and Curie temperature, including a breakdown of de Gennes scaling below a rare-earth-content threshold. This is a materials discovery study, not evidence of a finished permanent magnet or deployed technology.

26 Jul 2023CMS Collaboration; CERN

CMS makes the first hadron-collider observation of boosted Z→cc̄ decay

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In 138 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton-proton data, CMS observed boosted Z→cc̄ production in association with jets for the first time at a hadron collider. The measured signal strength relative to the Standard Model prediction is 1.00 with combined uncertainty +0.19/−0.17. A deep neural network (DeepDoubleX) identified large-radius jets from charm-quark pairs; the paper also sets a 95% confidence-level upper limit of 47 times the Standard Model prediction on boosted H→cc̄ production.

Technology: DeepDoubleX deep neural network for double-charm jet tagging

Editorial relevance 60 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The neural network processed secondary-vertex and particle-flow candidate features to classify boosted two-prong jets as charm versus light flavor and charm versus bottom. Its charm tagging separated the Z→cc̄ signal from dominant multijet backgrounds and supported the first observation in the boosted high-transverse-momentum regime.
Test and result
In 138 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton-proton data, CMS observed boosted Z→cc̄ production in association with jets for the first time at a hadron collider.
Limitation / still unknown
The first-observation claim is specifically for Z→cc̄ in association with jets at high transverse momentum at a hadron collider; it is not the first observation of Z decay generally. The neural tagger is an important analysis tool, while the observation derives from the full CMS event selection and likelihood fit. In the same paper, boosted H→cc̄ was not observed; only an upper limit is reported.

17 Jul 2023Zhejiang Cancer Hospital; Shanghai Jiao Tong University and six collaborating clinical centers

Machine-learning analysis of plasma metabolomic fingerprints yields a 21-metabolite gastric-cancer panel validated across multicentre cohorts

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In a multicentre study, machine-learning analysis of plasma metabolic fingerprints from 1,944 retrospective participants selected a 21-metabolite panel that differentiated gastric-cancer from non-cancer cohorts; five model types achieved AUCs of 0.921–0.971 in discovery and 0.907–0.940 in external verification. Prospective testing in 264 participants using both NPELDI-MS and UPLC-MS reported diagnostic AUCs of 0.855–0.918 and 0.856–0.916, respectively. The paper also reports a four-metabolite prognostic score based on retrospective survival data.

Technology: Neural network, lasso regression, ridge regression, support-vector machine and random-forest models applied to plasma metabolic fingerprints

Editorial relevance 67 medicine Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: prospective or independent check. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The study used machine-learning feature selection/modeling (including logistic-regression coefficient filtering) to identify and evaluate a 21-metabolite diagnostic panel from high-throughput mass-spectrometry fingerprints; classifiers were then validated on independent retrospective and prospective cohorts.
Test and result
In a multicentre study, machine-learning analysis of plasma metabolic fingerprints from 1,944 retrospective participants selected a 21-metabolite panel that differentiated gastric-cancer from non-cancer cohorts; five model types achieved AUCs of 0.921–0.971 in discovery and 0.907–0.940 in external verification.
Limitation / still unknown
The panel was evaluated in selected clinical cohorts and is not established for routine screening or clinical use. Prospective validation was at one lead center, and only six panel metabolites were robustly detected by confirmatory UPLC-MS under the reported testing. Model performance does not show that the panel is causal or improves outcomes. Primary full text/abstract checked; no independent replication assessed.

3 Jul 2023University of California, Los Angeles; Stanford University; EarlyDiagnostics; University of Southern California; University of California, San Diego

Deep learning detects elevated tissue-derived cfDNA across cancer and cirrhosis cohorts

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A study built a fragment-level methylation atlas from 521 noncancer tissue samples spanning 29 human tissue types and used it with cfSort, a supervised deep neural network, to estimate tissue composition in plasma cell-free DNA. Applying cfSort to patient data, the investigators found higher cfDNA fractions from the affected tissue in liver, lung, colorectal and stomach cancer and cirrhosis cohorts than in healthy controls; the estimated fractions also increased with cancer stage. The tissue-origin signal was reproduced on an independent whole-genome-bisulfite-sequencing dataset for liver cancer. In serial samples from four non-small-cell lung cancer patients receiving anti-PD-1 therapy, estimated liver or kidney tissue fractions tracked measured biochemical markers of organ injury.

Technology: cfSort, an ensemble of two dense deep neural networks trained on simulated mixtures using a fragment-level tissue methylation atlas

Editorial relevance 53 medicine Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The team used the supervised cfSort model to deconvolve methylation profiles in plasma cfDNA and estimate contributions from 29 tissue types. This enabled the reported comparison of affected-tissue cfDNA fractions in cancer and cirrhosis patient cohorts versus controls and longitudinal tracking of tissue-injury signals during immunotherapy. The atlas and model were also evaluated against simulated mixtures and independent methylation data.
Test and result
Applying cfSort to patient data, the investigators found higher cfDNA fractions from the affected tissue in liver, lung, colorectal and stomach cancer and cirrhosis cohorts than in healthy controls; the estimated fractions also increased with cancer stage.
Limitation / still unknown
The disease cohorts were retrospective and the clinical analyses demonstrate association and measurement feasibility, not prospective diagnostic benefit or improved outcomes. The clinical groups were 21 cirrhosis patients and 201 cancer patients, with 100 healthy controls; cross-platform liver-cancer validation used 24 cases and 32 controls. Anti-PD-1 injury monitoring involved only four NSCLC patients at three timepoints. The authors note that disease-specific methylation markers could improve cancer detection beyond tissue-of-origin fractions.

28 Jun 2023University of Toronto; King Abdullah University of Science and Technology (KAUST)

Machine-learning-guided experiments identify a high-power-factor PbSe:SnSb thermoelectric family

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A closed-loop error-correction learning workflow guided synthesis of PbSe alloyed with SnSb and documented a previously unexplored low-temperature thermoelectric family. At 300 K, the best tested composition, 2 wt% SnSb-doped PbSe, had a measured power factor above 850 µW mK⁻² and more than twice that of PbSe. Density-functional calculations attribute the modeled transport improvement to Sn/Sb interactions that reduce hole effective mass.

Technology: Closed-loop error-correction learning with a neural-network update to a prior materials-property model; composition representations compared included Magpie, Roost and CrabNet

Editorial relevance 53 materials Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A model trained on published experimental thermoelectric data ranked candidate compositions, and a dense neural-network error-correction model incorporated local experimental comparisons to adapt predictions to the authors’ synthesis and measurement conditions. Iterative model-guided experiments selected the PbSe:SnSb family and 2 wt% SnSb-doped PbSe for characterization. AI supplied composition down-selection and adaptive experiment guidance; the authors synthesized and measured the samples.
Test and result
A closed-loop error-correction learning workflow guided synthesis of PbSe alloyed with SnSb and documented a previously unexplored low-temperature thermoelectric family.
Limitation / still unknown
The result is specific to the tested powder-processing, composition and measurement protocol, including synthesis below 300 °C; it is not evidence of device-level or commercial performance. The reported mechanism for the increased power factor is supported by DFT calculations, not a direct measurement of carrier effective mass. The paper compares the 2 wt% composition with PbSe and reports more than 2× power factor at room temperature.

21 Jun 2023Lawrence Livermore National Laboratory

CogSim helps predict fusion ignition at the NIF

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Neural networks, Bayesian inference and physics simulations helped assess, before the experiment, the NIF design that produced 3.15 MJ of fusion energy from 2.05 MJ of laser energy delivered to the target on December 5, 2022. Experts designed the experiment; AI estimated its outcome and uncertainties. This is not the energy balance of the entire facility; the study was later published in Science in 2025.

Technology: CogSim (neural networks, transfer learning and Bayesian inference)

Editorial relevance 73 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A generative model combining simulations, deep learning, experimental data and Bayesian statistics estimated the outcome and uncertainties of the NIF design before the shot.
Test and result
The CogSim team predicted the December 5, 2022 shot outcome before the experiment (about 50.2% chance of exceeding break-even), and the result of 3.15 MJ from 2.05 MJ matched the prediction after peer review and independent diagnostics.
Limitation / still unknown
The page says the approach does not guarantee success and that techniques still have much to prove; one good prediction does not validate a model, and testing on more experiments was planned for 2023.

12 Jun 2023Gladstone Institutes; University of California, San Francisco; Chan Zuckerberg Biohub San Francisco

SIMMER-guided experiments identify gut bacterial strains that deplete methotrexate

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: SIMMER combines reaction-chemistry and protein-sequence similarity to predict gut microbial species and enzymes responsible for drug biotransformations. For methotrexate (MTX), the model predicted candidate strains and enzyme sequences; researchers tested 42 gut bacterial isolates by HPLC. SIMMER predicted 13 isolates as MTX-metabolizing, and 10 of those tested isolates showed MTX depletion; the association was significant in the study (Fisher exact p=0.046). Predicted MTX-hydrolysis enzyme signatures were also enriched in stool samples of MTX nonresponders in an analyzed rheumatoid-arthritis cohort.

Technology: SIMMER, a computational reaction-similarity and protein-homology pipeline using chemical fingerprints, MetaCyc reactions and gut metagenomic sequences

Editorial relevance 53 biology Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
SIMMER proposed specific bacterial species and enzyme sequences for known drug transformations. The team tested its MTX predictions against an independent panel of 42 gut bacterial isolates and HPLC-measured drug depletion, and compared predicted enzyme presence with baseline stool metagenomes and later MTX response in an RA dataset.
Test and result
For methotrexate (MTX), the model predicted candidate strains and enzyme sequences; researchers tested 42 gut bacterial isolates by HPLC.
Limitation / still unknown
The study reports species-level evidence for bacterial MTX depletion, but the enzyme predictions themselves were not individually purified or functionally validated. Only 13 of 42 tested isolates were predicted positive and 10 depleted MTX; predictions may have false positives, as the authors note. The RA association is observational and does not establish that microbial metabolism caused treatment nonresponse. The underlying fact that gut bacteria can metabolize MTX was already known; this result concerns species-level prediction and validation, not discovery of MTX metabolism itself.

12 Jun 2023ATLAS Collaboration; CERN

ATLAS graph neural network enables first observation of four-top-quark production

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Using 140 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton–proton collisions, ATLAS observed simultaneous production of four top quarks in same-sign dilepton and multilepton events, with 6.1σ observed (4.3σ expected) significance. The measured cross-section is 22.5⁺⁶·⁶₋₅·₅ fb, consistent with the Standard Model prediction within 1.8σ. A graph neural network separated the four-top signal from dominant backgrounds in the signal extraction.

Technology: Event-level graph neural network built with graph_nets/TensorFlow

Editorial relevance 60 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The GNN discriminant classified events using a fully connected graph of reconstructed jets, leptons and missing transverse momentum, with node, edge and global features. It separated four-top signal from background and supplied the discriminant used in the signal extraction and cross-section fit.
Test and result
Using 140 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton–proton collisions, ATLAS observed simultaneous production of four top quarks in same-sign dilepton and multilepton events, with 6.1σ observed (4.3σ expected) significance.
Limitation / still unknown
This is an ATLAS observation from its own 140 fb⁻¹ analysis after earlier ATLAS/CMS evidence; it is one paper-level observation, not a separate count for CMS or later combinations. The measured cross-section uncertainty is large and its central value is 1.8σ above the SM prediction, so this is not evidence of a significant deviation from the Standard Model. Background control regions and systematic uncertainties are part of the analysis.

7 Jun 2023Google DeepMind

AlphaDev: reinforcement learning discovers faster sorting routines

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Working at the assembly-instruction level, the system found sorting routines up to 70% faster for sequences of 3–5 elements and about 1.7% faster for sequences over 250,000 elements; they were adopted in the LLVM libc++ standard library. A hashing algorithm 30% faster was added to Abseil. The largest gains are for short sequences.

Technology: AlphaDev (reinforcement learning)

Family AlphaDev: profile · models page

Editorial relevance 60 computer science Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
AlphaDev, an AlphaZero-based reinforcement learning agent with a transformer encoder, built assembly programs instruction by instruction, rewarded for correctness and latency; the team translated results to C++.
Test and result
AlphaDev found sort 3 and sort 5 routines with fewer instructions than human benchmarks, matched sort 4, and had lower latency on variable sorts. Libc++ versions were up to 70% faster for five elements and about 1.7% faster above 250,000 elements.
Limitation / still unknown
Hashing result (30% faster) not seen in the text read. Search is at assembly level; the paper reports optimality proof only for sort 3 (17 instructions). Results cover tested CPUs: ARMv8, Intel Skylake, AMD Zen 2.

31 May 2023Dana-Farber Cancer Institute; Broad Institute of MIT and Harvard; Massachusetts General Hospital

Geneformer identifies cardiac gene targets validated in engineered heart tissues

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Geneformer used learned single-cell transcriptome representations to nominate genes affecting cardiac function. Laboratory tests found that loss of the predicted dosage-sensitive candidate TEAD4 reduced contractile stress in cardiac microtissues. In a separate disease-model application, targeting the Geneformer-nominated genes GSN or PLN improved contractile stress in microtissues carrying a TTN truncating mutation. These are same-study findings in engineered cell-derived tissues, not evidence of clinical benefit. The date records the journal online version; an earlier public version was not established in the bounded search.

It learned separate growth rules for each organism and showed that the closely related species have different amino-acid auxotrophies. For S. gordonii, its neural-network growth predictions exceeded 90% accuracy after nine days and 3,024 experiments; the article also reports transfer-learning tests in larger media.

Source check: Needs source check. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Wong et al. screened 2,352 compounds for senolytic activity in etoposide-induced senescence, trained graph neural networks on the results and scored more than 800,000 molecules. Machine-learning classification shows rigidity-corrected moment release is similar across earthquake depths

28 Apr 2023University of Szeged; Tanta University

Learned satellite mapping characterizes mixed floating-debris accumulation along the Tisza

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A support-vector classifier applied to 61 Sentinel-2 images mapped mixed natural and human-made floating-debris patches along 175 kilometers of the Middle Tisza River. The estimates identified a large persistent accumulation upstream of Kisköre Dam and changes across years and river stages. Moderate test performance, coarse pixels and biased visibility of large patches limit these estimates; they are not a plastic-mass inventory or proof that the dam caused the pattern.

Technology: RBF support-vector classification of multispectral satellite pixels

Editorial relevance 40 climate Original source Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The learned SVC separated debris-like from non-debris pixel signatures across repeated satellite observations, enabling the represented regional spatial and temporal accumulation analysis beyond the detector leaderboard.
Test and result
Training/validation used 1,974 labeled satellite pixels with an 80:20 random split; three separate litter-patch regions were used for size/generalization testing against higher-resolution imagery. The selected SVC had F1 approximately 0.62 on that broader test, below its random-pixel validation result. The model was then applied to 61 images from 2015–2021 across a 175-kilometer reach to estimate patch distributions and temporal changes.
Limitation / still unknown
Mixed debris includes substantial natural vegetation and wood; artificial litter cannot be separately quantified. Labels emphasized large persistent accumulations, and reference imagery differed in acquisition time. Spatially correlated pixels, tuning on limited data, false positives from vegetation/shadows/structures and missed sub-pixel patches constrain estimates. Three patch regions do not establish broad independent geographic validation. Temporal map patterns are model-derived observations, without independent field verification of every mapped patch or causal attribution to dam operation.

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A synthetic genetic-interaction and cell-morphology map covering more than 6,800 Drosophila genes supported machine-learning-based gene-function assignments across 47 modules. In follow-up, Cytoclass single-cell classification revealed a state-specific interaction between Cdk2 and COP9 signalosome knockdown: co-depletion arrested cell-cycle progression and produced a senescence-like phenotype with increased antimicrobial-peptide expression in cultured cells and hemocytes, including without bacterial infection.

Technology: Single-cell morphology classifier ensemble and machine-learning analysis of genetic-interaction profiles (Cytoclass; support-vector machines and random forest)

Editorial relevance 60 biology Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The authors generated single-cell morphology and synthetic genetic-interaction profiles, used machine-learning-based function discovery to assign functions to genes in 47 modules, and applied Cytoclass state classification to reveal interactions hidden in bulk-average profiles. The state-specific Cdk2–COP9 interaction was followed up by double-RNAi assays, single-cell transcriptomics, beta-galactosidase and antimicrobial-peptide measurements.
Test and result
A synthetic genetic-interaction and cell-morphology map covering more than 6,800 Drosophila genes supported machine-learning-based gene-function assignments across 47 modules.
Limitation / still unknown
The findings are from cultured Drosophila cells and hemocytes; they do not establish mammalian or human disease mechanisms. The senescence-like phenotype and antimicrobial-peptide induction were measured after experimental RNAi co-depletion. The global module assignments and many network interactions are computational inferences; only selected interactions received targeted follow-up.

25 Apr 2023Icahn School of Medicine at Mount Sinai; University of Maryland School of Medicine; Hospital for Special Surgery

Longitudinal EHR machine learning identifies patients who may need autoimmune rheumatic-disease evaluation years earlier

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A random-forest model trained on longitudinal electronic health records from 161,584 people across two institutions identified records associated with high-specificity autoantibody testing for systemic autoimmune rheumatic diseases (SARDs). The model achieved AUROC 0.93 in internal validation and 0.87 on the external All of Us cohort. In a separate BioMe cohort, model probabilities tracked SARD and other autoimmune diagnoses; records could predict later autoantibody testing and rheumatology encounters up to five years before those events. The study also found higher autoantibody yield among model-prioritized individuals than under the comparison clinical-testing rate.

Technology: Random-forest machine-learning model using longitudinal electronic-health-record diagnoses, medications, laboratory results and vital signs

Editorial relevance 47 medicine Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The model ranked patients by probability of receiving SARD-specific autoantibody testing, using clinical data preceding the test or rheumatology encounter. Investigators evaluated performance in internal validation, the external All of Us cohort and an independent BioMe cohort, and tested whether higher scores tracked diagnosed autoimmune disease and identified later testing or encounters earlier in retrospective records.
Test and result
A random-forest model trained on longitudinal electronic health records from 161,584 people across two institutions identified records associated with high-specificity autoantibody testing for systemic autoimmune rheumatic diseases (SARDs).
Limitation / still unknown
The model predicts clinical suspicion and need for rheumatological testing, not a SARD diagnosis. The study was retrospective and opportunistic; autoantibody testing and rheumatology encounters are imperfect proxies for clinical need, and some diseases were not included in the training target. The authors state prospective studies are needed to determine whether model-guided testing improves care or outcomes. The five-year lead is retrospective prediction of later evaluation events, not proven earlier diagnosis or patient benefit.

18 Apr 2023Dublin Institute for Advanced Studies; Northeastern University; Stanford University

Machine learning finds ribbon-knot certificates and rules out candidate pairs

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Bayesian optimization and reinforcement learning search for sequences of knot moves that certify a knot is ribbon. The primary v1 reports that both knots in 843 specified pairs were shown ribbon, eliminating those pairs as potential counterexamples in the studied strategy. Hydrodynamic simulations of a system containing a 5-Jupiter-mass planet at 75 au reproduced the observed kinematic structure; the authors conclude that HD 142666 hosts an embedded planet by current field criteria. The planet is inferred from disk kinematics rather than directly imaged.
