29 Mar 2023University of Texas at Austin; and Ames National Laboratory

Machine-learning-guided calculations predict four rare-earth-free magnetic Fe–Co–Si compounds

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A crystal-graph neural network screened 357,480 hypothetical Fe–Co–Si structures; adaptive genetic search and density-functional calculations then identified four predicted ternary structures with calculated saturation polarization ≥1.0 T, easy-axis anisotropy ≥1.0 MJ/m³, and Curie temperature >840 K. Their calculated energies lie within 70 meV/atom of the ternary convex hull, supporting but not proving experimental accessibility. The paper proposes the structures for synthesis; it does not experimentally demonstrate them.

Technology: Crystal graph convolutional neural network (CGCNN) with an adaptive genetic algorithm

Editorial relevance 47 materials Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
CGCNN models rapidly screened hypothetical structures and predicted formation energy and magnetic polarization; first-principles results were fed back to train improved models across successive generations, while an adaptive genetic algorithm searched lower-energy structures. ML materially narrowed the space for the final density-functional magnetic-property calculations.
Test and result
A crystal-graph neural network screened 357,480 hypothetical Fe–Co–Si structures; adaptive genetic search and density-functional calculations then identified four predicted ternary structures with calculated saturation polarization ≥1.0 T, easy-axis anisotropy ≥1.0 MJ/m³, and Curie temperature >840 K.
Limitation / still unknown
This is a theoretical, ML-guided first-principles prediction; the paper proposes the structures for synthesis and does not report experimental synthesis. The abstract describes four compounds; the results section mentions five structures meeting initial thresholds, of which four are dynamically stable. Report the four final candidates and their calculated properties only. The work establishes neither actual permanent-magnet performance nor synthesizability.

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A linear support-vector machine distinguished focused-attention from open-monitoring fMRI connectivity patterns in twelve experienced monks, with reported internal accuracy of 65.6%; classification was not significant in ten novice meditators. 23 Mar 2023University of Science and Technology of China

Learned catalyst search guides oxygen-evolution catalysts made from meteorites

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A robotic chemistry system combined a neural-network performance model with theoretical calculations and experimental calibration to select a catalyst formulation from five Martian meteorite feedstocks. Laboratory testing of the selected formulation measured an oxygen-evolution overpotential of 445.1 mV at 10 mA per square centimetre, lower than the compared theory-only and experiment-only search formulations. The work demonstrates a terrestrial laboratory catalyst result; oxygen production on Mars and a globally optimal composition were not independently established.

1 Mar 2023Los Alamos National Laboratory; Argonne National Laboratory

Adaptive learning finds higher-activity Fe–N–C oxygen-reduction catalysts

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: An adaptive-learning design-of-experiments loop guided robotic high-throughput synthesis of Fe–N–C oxygen-reduction electrocatalysts across a six-dimensional process/composition space. Starting from 36 characterized samples, the team synthesized 14 additional catalyst formulations from a nominal search space of roughly 10 billion combinations. Four outperformed the best sample in the initial dataset; the best showed about 33% higher ORR activity, reported as 16.3 mA mg⁻¹ at 0.8 V in rotating-disk measurements.

Technology: Adaptive learning framework with machine-learning surrogates, statistical inference, uncertainty quantification, and global optimization, paired with robotic high-throughput synthesis

Editorial relevance 53 materials Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The model-based design-of-experiments tool used current measurements and uncertainty to select subsequent synthesis conditions in a six-dimensional search space. It guided the selection of 14 samples for synthesis and measurement, producing four catalysts that exceeded the initial best. Robotic high-throughput synthesis and RRDE testing generated the experimental feedback; researchers interpreted the results.
Test and result
An adaptive-learning design-of-experiments loop guided robotic high-throughput synthesis of Fe–N–C oxygen-reduction electrocatalysts across a six-dimensional process/composition space.
Limitation / still unknown
The finding concerns optimized Fe–N–C catalyst formulations and synthesis conditions within the study’s defined six-dimensional search; it is not discovery of a new catalyst family or proof that all approximately 10 billion combinations were tested. The 33% improvement is relative to the best sample in the initial 36-sample dataset. Activity was measured using rotating-ring disk electrode testing, not a fuel-cell device or long-duration operating demonstration. The study reports 14 new synthesized catalysts and four that beat the initial samples; these are one paper-level result, not separate entries.

27 Feb 2023Sapienza University of Rome; Sant’Andrea University Hospital

Clinical coronary CT comparison finds medium-strength learned reconstruction gives the clearest plaque images

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In 51 patients, a paired comparison of fourteen reconstructions per coronary CT scan found that medium-strength deep learning reconstruction received the highest plaque-image quality ratings. High-strength reconstruction reduced noise most, but readers noted blurring of small plaque contours. The study tested image quality, without invasive angiography as a diagnostic reference or demonstrated clinical outcome benefit.

Technology: TrueFidelity deep neural network reconstruction trained on high-quality filtered-back-projection images

Editorial relevance 47 biology Original source Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The pretrained neural reconstruction selectively suppressed image noise in actual patient acquisitions, enabling the paired clinical test of image-quality and plaque-contour tradeoffs across reconstruction strengths.
Test and result
Prospective single-center enrollment yielded 51 patients and 714 reconstructed datasets. Each acquisition was reconstructed using filtered back-projection, ten ASiR-V strengths and three learned reconstruction strengths. Two experienced radiologists, blinded to reconstruction protocol, assessed four selected reconstructions in consensus, with randomized reading order and separated sessions. Medium-strength DLIR had the highest subjective plaque-image quality; high-strength DLIR had lower noise but did not significantly outperform maximum-strength ASiR-V on the reported objective noise, SNR and CNR comparisons.
Limitation / still unknown
One vendor and center, selected subjective comparison datasets, consensus readers without separate-reader reproducibility, and exclusion of severe motion or high heart rates limit generalization. No invasive coronary reference was routinely available, so agreement between image reconstructions does not establish diagnostic accuracy. No radiation-dose reduction, patient outcome benefit or dedicated stent subgroup result was tested.

13 Feb 2023IBM Research–Almaden; IBM Research–Zurich; ETH Zurich; Arctoris

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A regression-transformer model fine-tuned on historical ring-opening-polymerization data generated candidate catalysts. One surrogate, 5c, reached 60% conversion in 32 minutes for monomer 2a and 100% conversion in 45 minutes for monomer 2d, with reported dispersities of 1.06 and 1.34, respectively. The experimental results appeared in ChemRxiv v2, posted 2023-02-13, and the 2023 peer-reviewed journal article followed on 2023-06-21. The measured tests concern modified surrogates, not the unmodified generated structure.

A regression transformer generated catalyst 3g from historical polymerization data; researchers tested synthetically accessible modified surrogates 5c and 5d that preserved its endocyclic guanidine motif.
Test and result
The journal reports model-derived catalyst 3g and synthetically accessible modified surrogates 5c/5d. Surrogate 5c produced 60% conversion of monomer 2a in 32 minutes (dispersity 1.06) and 100% conversion of monomer 2d in 45 minutes (dispersity 1.34).
Limitation / still unknown
The tested catalysts were modified surrogates rather than the unmodified model-generated 3g structure. These are laboratory ring-opening-polymerization outcomes, not process-scale or commercial performance. The earlier 2022 v1 reported model generation and literature-known catalyst recovery; the experimentally validated result is reported in v2 on 2023-02-13 and in the later journal article.

8 Feb 2023Washington University in St. Louis; collaborating institutions

CellOracle-guided zebrafish perturbations identify axial-mesoderm effects of noto and lhx1a loss

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: The CellOracle machine-learning workflow used single-cell gene-regulatory networks to prioritize transcription factors for perturbation in zebrafish development. CRISPR/Cas9 tests confirmed a model-predicted, previously unreported increase in prechordal-plate differentiation after loss of noto, alongside the known loss of notochord. Model-prioritized lhx1a loss also inhibited axial-mesoderm differentiation: single-cell RNA-seq found expansion of early notochord cells and reductions in later notochord and prechordal-plate populations; orthogonal RNA assays supported reduced notochord/prechordal markers.

Technology: CellOracle, a machine-learning gene-regulatory-network model with in-silico transcription-factor perturbation

Editorial relevance 60 biology Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The model built context-specific networks from single-cell data, simulated transcription-factor loss, and prioritized noto and lhx1a for experimental follow-up. Researchers used CRISPR/Cas9 crispants, single-cell RNA-seq and marker assays to test the predicted zebrafish cell-fate effects; the prechordal-plate increase after noto loss was reported as previously unrecognized.
Test and result
CRISPR/Cas9 tests confirmed a model-predicted, previously unreported increase in prechordal-plate differentiation after loss of noto, alongside the known loss of notochord.
Limitation / still unknown
Evidence is from zebrafish crispants during embryonic development, not stable mutants or mammalian/human systems; the authors report no gross morphological change despite transcriptomic/cell-population effects. The CellOracle model depends on inferred networks and makes cell-state-shift predictions, not direct causal proof of all inferred edges. A later unreviewed 2024 preprint raises implementation and benchmarking concerns, particularly about its use of ATAC-seq for distal regulatory interactions and comparisons with other GRN tools; this record relies only on the specific perturbation predictions tested experimentally and does not adopt the paper's broader benchmarking claims.

4 Feb 2023Miami University; University of Kansas

Unsupervised machine learning reveals previously missed hydraulic-fracturing seismicity in Live Oak County

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: An unsupervised repeating-signal detector, combined with template matching, exposed small earthquake bursts in a region of the Eagle Ford shale where conventional catalogs had detected little seismicity. The authors report that the new events temporally coincided with hydraulic-fracturing activity, and their regional analysis found detectable seismicity began after injection flux exceeded about two million barrels per month despite similar cumulative injection volume; this supports, but does not prove, a causal role for injection rate and is limited by sparse station coverage.

Technology: Repeating Signal Detector (RSD), an unsupervised machine-learning waveform-family detector

Editorial relevance 47 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
RSD searched continuous seismograms without relying on preselected earthquake templates, clustered similar signals, and revealed small-magnitude event bursts missed by the previous catalog. Researchers manually inspected signals and analyzed their timing relative to hydraulic-fracturing records.
Test and result
An unsupervised repeating-signal detector, combined with template matching, exposed small earthquake bursts in a region of the Eagle Ford shale where conventional catalogs had detected little seismicity.
Limitation / still unknown
The RSD detections were manually inspected and some event locations were poorly constrained; the authors note station density limits detection and do not establish causation from temporal correlation alone. This paper extends an earlier Eagle Ford study but reports a distinct Live Oak County seismicity region and subsequent time-period catalog.

27 Jan 2023Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health; Atherosclerosis Risk in Communities study centers; National University of Singapore; New York University Grossman School of Medicine; collaborating institutions

Elastic-net proteomic risk modeling and multi-cohort validation identify proteins associated with incident diabetes

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: An untargeted analysis of 4,955 plasma proteins in the ARIC cohort identified 140 associations with incident diabetes; 64 were significant in an internal sample and 47 (including 22 reported as novel) were associated in the external Singapore Multi-Ethnic Cohort. Elastic-net Cox modeling selected a 27-protein panel for 20-year risk prediction. In internal validation, adding those proteins to conventional risk factors including fasting glucose and HbA1c increased the C statistic from 0.720 to 0.731. Genetic-instrument analyses supported possible causal roles for SHBG, ATP1B2 and GSTA1, although ATP1B2’s genetic and observational directions differed.

Technology: Elastic-net Cox regression for protein feature selection and incident-diabetes risk prediction

Editorial relevance 47 medicine Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Elastic net selected a 27-protein combination from candidate proteins to improve 20-year incident-diabetes risk prediction beyond clinical and cardiometabolic covariates. The model was trained in the ARIC discovery subset and internally evaluated in a held-out ARIC subset. Separate Cox association and genetic-instrument analyses reported the externally validated protein associations; the record does not attribute those analyses to machine learning.
Test and result
An untargeted analysis of 4,955 plasma proteins in the ARIC cohort identified 140 associations with incident diabetes; 64 were significant in an internal sample and 47 (including 22 reported as novel) were associated in the external Singapore Multi-Ethnic Cohort.
Limitation / still unknown
The 47-protein associations were externally tested in Singapore MEC; the elastic-net predictive model itself was internally validated in ARIC, not independently externally validated. Its incremental C statistic over covariates including fasting glucose and HbA1c was modest (0.011; P=0.04), and the protein model overestimated observed diabetes risk across internal-validation deciles. The causal claims are genetic-instrument inferences, not experimental proof; ATP1B2 had opposite directions in genetic-instrument versus observational analyses. Cohort associations do not establish clinical utility.

21 Jan 2023Stanford Health Care; Yale University; California Department of Public Health

Random-forest metabolite profiling reduces false-positive newborn screens for four inherited disorders

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A 121-analyte LC–MS/MS panel combined established markers, untargeted-metabolomics features and chromatographically resolved isobars with random-forest classification of California newborn-screen dried blood spots. In 883 archived infant samples, the study reports that the second-tier approach maintained 100% sensitivity while reducing false positives by 83% for glutaric acidemia type I, 84% for methylmalonic acidemia, 100% for ornithine transcarbamylase deficiency and 51% for very-long-chain acyl-CoA dehydrogenase deficiency. Several long-chain acylcarnitine and hydroxy-acylcarnitine isobar features contributed to classification, although their chemical identities were unresolved.

Technology: Random Forest classifier trained on targeted LC–MS/MS metabolite profiles

Editorial relevance 47 medicine Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The classifier modeled multianalyte profiles from true-positive and false-positive screen-positive samples to distinguish the groups for four inherited metabolic disorders. Feature-importance analysis showed that complex profiles, including unresolved long-chain acylcarnitine/hydroxy-acylcarnitine isobars, contributed to classification; the model's second-tier performance was assessed against known case status.
Test and result
In 883 archived infant samples, the study reports that the second-tier approach maintained 100% sensitivity while reducing false positives by 83% for glutaric acidemia type I, 84% for methylmalonic acidemia, 100% for ornithine transcarbamylase deficiency and 51% for very-long-chain acyl-CoA dehydrogenase deficiency.
Limitation / still unknown
This was a proof-of-principle second-tier simulation on residual archived California newborn-screen specimens, not a prospective implementation study. The validation sample was not a fully independent external set, and archived-sample degradation and processing batch effects limited biomarker discovery; some discriminating isobar identities and biological significance remain unknown. The reported sensitivity and false-positive reductions are cohort-specific and do not establish improved health outcomes or routine clinical utility.

20 Jan 2023MIT; Mass General Cancer Center and Chang Gung Memorial Hospital

Sybil: lung-cancer risk prediction up to six years from a low-dose CT

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: The model predicts risk from a single CT with a six-year C-index of 0.75 (NLST), 0.81 (MGH) and 0.80 (CGMH), and a one-year ROC area between 0.86 and 0.94. Retrospective validation on three independent datasets; trained only on smokers, with prospective testing and testing in nonsmokers as next steps.

Technology: Sybil (neural network on CT scans)

Editorial relevance 60 medicine Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A deep learning model predicts lung-cancer risk from a single low-dose chest CT.
Test and result
The model was trained on NLST low-dose CT scans and validated on a held-out NLST set and two independent cohorts (MGH and CGMH), with six-year C-index 0.75, 0.81 and 0.80; the abstract describes no prospective testing.
Limitation / still unknown
The abstract states that future study is required to understand clinical applications; the press release says prospective testing and testing in nonsmokers are next steps.

19 Jan 2023University of Toronto; Insilico Medicine and collaborators

A liver-cancer target and molecule identified with Pharma.AI

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Pharma.AI combined PandaOmics, Chemistry42 and an AlphaFold-predicted structure to identify a hepatocellular-carcinoma target and produce a hit molecule within 30 days of target selection, synthesizing seven compounds. A second round produced a more potent hit; an early Chemical Science study, with no clinical efficacy demonstrated.

Technology: Pharma.AI, PandaOmics, Chemistry42 and AlphaFold

Family AlphaFold: profile · models page

Editorial relevance 60 medicine Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Pharma.AI platforms (PandaOmics, Chemistry42) used AlphaFold-predicted structures to select a liver-cancer target and generate candidate molecules.
Test and result
Selected compounds were synthesized and tested in biological assays: the first hit bound CDK20 (Kd about 9.2 µM) and a second AI-driven round gave a more potent compound with greater antiproliferative activity in liver cancer cells; the abstract describes no in vivo testing.
Limitation / still unknown
The abstract notes AlphaFold predictions have varying confidence, the first hit came from only 7 synthesized compounds, and potency values vary widely; no clinical efficacy is demonstrated.

13 Jan 2023Brookhaven National Laboratory Center for Functional Nanomaterials and National Synchrotron Light Source II; Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory CAMERA

Autonomous X-ray mapping reveals previously unseen block-copolymer morphologies, including a nanoscale ladder

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: An autonomous experiment planner selected synchrotron X-ray measurements across a combinatorial block-copolymer blend sample. The resulting map and targeted electron microscopy revealed previously unreported alternating, skew/bilayer, and ladder-like self-assembled morphologies, and the authors infer a general mechanism of local subdomain partitioning.

Technology: gpCAM autonomous experimentation framework for selecting high-value measurements

Editorial relevance 60 materials Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The algorithm autonomously chose where to collect synchrotron X-ray scattering data and updated its model online; researchers then used electron microscopy to validate and interpret the novel morphologies.
Test and result
An autonomous experiment planner selected synchrotron X-ray measurements across a combinatorial block-copolymer blend sample.
Limitation / still unknown
The newly discovered results are nanoscale morphologies in one specific polymer-blend/template system; the paper does not show that each morphology is already useful in a device. AI selected measurements, while domain researchers performed electron-microscopy confirmation and interpretation.

12 Jan 2023Zhejiang University

Machine-learning pipeline identifies antimicrobial hexapeptides

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A sequential machine-learning pipeline screened virtual libraries of 6–9-residue peptides and identified three leading antimicrobial hexapeptides. The paper reports activity against multidrug-resistant clinical isolates and therapeutic effects in a mouse bacterial-pneumonia model. These are laboratory and animal results, not clinical efficacy.

Technology: Sequential machine-learning pipeline for peptide selection, classification, ranking and activity regression

Editorial relevance 60 biology Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Sequential learned modules selected, classified, ranked and regressed peptide candidates from the virtual sequence library; the reported three lead hexapeptides are outputs of that workflow.
Test and result
The authors report a sequential machine-learning pipeline over virtual 6–9 amino-acid sequence space; the leading three identified hexapeptides were tested against clinical multidrug-resistant isolates and in a mouse bacterial-pneumonia model.
Limitation / still unknown
The mouse model and reported comparison with penicillin do not establish human efficacy. The PubMed primary metadata gives journal online date 2023-01-12; no earlier pre-2020 version is identified in the reviewed primary material.

12 Jan 2023Lunenfeld-Tanenbaum Research Institute, Mount Sinai Hospital

AlphaFold2-Multimer screen of the 53BP1 pathway predicts a RIF1-SHLD3 interface that is confirmed in cells

Source check: Needs source check. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Sifri et al. used AlphaFold2-Multimer to predict all pairwise interactions in the 53BP1-RIF1-shieldin-CST pathway, recovered seven known interfaces and predicted a new one between the RIF1 HEAT-repeat domain and the SHLD3 eIF4E-like domain. Pulldown and cellular assays supported the model and showed the interaction is needed for shieldin recruitment, class switch recombination and PARP inhibitor sensitivity. First public version: bioRxiv 2023-01-12; journal version 2023-06-12. Abstract-level review only.

Technology: AlphaFold2-Multimer

Family AlphaFold: profile · models page

Editorial relevance 60 biology Original source Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
AlphaFold2-Multimer predicted pairwise complexes and proposed the novel RIF1-SHLD3 interface that guided the experiments.
Test and result
The predicted interface was tested by in vitro pulldown and cellular assays, per the abstract.
Limitation / still unknown
Full Methods, mutant-design details and independent replication were not assessed; the 2023-01-12 date is the bioRxiv posting (10.1101/2023.01.12.523815), version differences not compared.

9 Jan 2023Absci

Zero-shot generative models design HER2 antibodies, with 71 low-nanomolar binders among 421 characterised

Source check: Needs source check. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Shanehsazzadeh et al. (Absci) described generative models for de novo antibody design against HER2 without further optimisation, combined with high-throughput screening of about 10^6 HCDR variants; binding rates were 10.6 percent (HCDR3) and 1.8 percent (HCDR123), 71 of 421 characterised binders had low-nanomolar affinity by SPR, 11 were functionally equivalent or better than trastuzumab, and one showed about 3x higher cell-based potency. Preprint, not peer-reviewed at this version; a later journal version should be checked. Abstract-level review.

Technology: Generative deep learning models for antibody design

Editorial relevance 73 medicine Original source Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Generative models proposed HCDR variants that were then screened for binding.
Test and result
Designed antibodies were expressed and measured by high-throughput screening and SPR, with comparison to trastuzumab (per abstract).
Limitation / still unknown
Peer review, in vivo activity, the reach beyond one target and the model details were not assessed; one organisation authored and tested the designs.

3 Jan 2023The Cooper Union for the Advancement of Science and Art; Manhattan College

Machine learning guides a proposed UCd3 intermetallic phase assignment

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A support-vector classifier using composition-derived elemental features predicted a Cu₃Au-type structure for UCd₃ and guided synthesis. Powder X-ray diffraction from the second of two samples showed peaks the authors fit to a UCd₃ phase with a = 4.648(2) Å; the sample also contained uranium, uranium oxide and cadmium, while the first sample differed. This is the authors’ proposed phase assignment from a multiphase powder, not phase-pure material or independent structural confirmation.

Technology: Support-vector machine discriminant analysis; Gaussian-process regression for feature completion

Editorial relevance 53 materials Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
SVM classification of composition-derived descriptors predicted a Cu₃Au-type structure and motivated experimental synthesis/testing.
Test and result
The authors report synthesis and powder X-ray diffraction; the proposed phase was present in the second of two samples.
Limitation / still unknown
Only one synthesis batch/sample (the second of two samples) showed the additional peaks assigned by the authors to UCd₃; the powder was multiphase. The checked 2022 primary review says UCd₁₁ was the only previously reported U-Cd compound, but this is not an independent confirmation of the proposed UCd₃ structure.
